# Agent Doc Completed Work

- 2026-05-13 [#donearchiveattr] Implement external completed-work archive support for agent:done using archive=<repo-relative>.done.md. Plan: tasks/agent-doc/plan-external-done-archive-attribute.md
- 2026-05-13 [#clearsessionmismatch] #agent-doc-bug [done]: Clear Session Context harness mismatch false positive — protected_clear_input_reason inferred wrong harness from document frontmatter instead of pane's actual command. Plan: tasks/agent-doc/plan-clear-session-harness-mismatch-false-positive.md
- 2026-05-13 [#tmuxcontrolmode] Migrate tmux-router to control mode (tmux -CC) for event-driven pane output and lifecycle notifications, replacing capture-pane polling. Natural fit for existing Tmux struct. [recommended]
- 2026-05-13 [#tmuxhybridpty] Implement hybrid control-mode + pipe-pane + owned-PTY architecture: control mode for lifecycle events, pipe-pane for output streaming, owned PTY only for managed panes (OpenCode, Claude) where input reliability matters. Depends on #tmuxcontrolmode.
- 2026-05-13 [#ownedpty] Implement portable-pty + alacritty_terminal backend for managed panes — agent-doc owns the child PTY directly instead of tmux send-keys/capture-pane, eliminating the input heuristic failure class. Depends on #multiplexertrait abstraction boundary. [recommended]
- 2026-05-13 [#startreadytimeout] #agent-doc-bug: Starting authoritative actors repeatedly miss the 8s route readiness window after owned/hybrid pty startup; logs show repeated route_authoritative_actor_starting_not_ready for Codex panes before eventual prompt readiness, and sometimes against a closed actor. Make starting-state routing fail closed with a single actionable diagnostic, refresh closed actors immediately, and cover with SimWorld plus tmux-router tests.
- 2026-05-13 [#multiplexertrait] Evaluate Multiplexer trait abstraction over tmux to reduce coupling (785 tmux references across 43 files). Would allow future Zellij backend and make the tmux surface testable via mock backend. [recommended]
- 2026-05-13 [#directsubmitbudget] #agent-doc-bug: Direct pane submit telemetry reports 5.18s timeouts / over-budget sends even when dispatch_start_proof later proves the prompt was consumed. Split input-acceptance latency from dispatch-start proof, avoid false timeout language after consumed proof, and tune the owned/hybrid pty submit budget around actual tmux/control-mode behavior.
- 2026-05-13 [#latefallbackloop] #agent-doc-bug: Late fallback patch writes keep firing after a cycle is already committed; logs show repeated late_fallback_patch_rejected reason=already_committed, ipc_write_consumed, commit_already_current, and one resume_commit_blocked_drift with an unresolved prompt-only tail. Stop fallback writers at terminal commit state or rebind them to the next cycle before accepting IPC patches.
- 2026-05-14 [#orchplainresp] #agent-doc-bug: Orchestration currently requires child template responses to emit patch:exchange for single-turn responses. Let orchestrate synthesize an exchange append for clean plain assistant output while still rejecting transcript-shaped, mixed patch/unmatched, full-document dump, or multi-component output without explicit structure. Add specs/tests and keep explicit patch blocks for intentional multi-component writes.
- 2026-05-14 [#clearstartingrace] #agent-doc-bug: Clear/restart commands can race starting owned panes; logs show session_clear_sent while generation 170 was still starting, followed by protected-input refusals, commits, and repeated starting_not_ready. Gate clear/restart on dispatch-ready or clean-exit prompt state and document-dirty state before sending literal Enter into the pane.
- 2026-05-14 [#ownedreapbusy] #agent-doc-bug: Owned pane reap decisions are noisy and often keep panes alive from spinner tails like Working/escape-title fragments instead of a stable idle/busy signal. Normalize screen-tail sampling or prefer supervisor prompt state so route_owned_reap_decision does not preserve panes based on transient renderer text.
- 2026-05-14 [#opencodeinputdiag] #agent-doc-bug: Add structured diagnostic logging for all tmux input paths — supervisor stdin forwarding, send-keys dispatch, Kitty keyboard protocol state, permission prompt detection transitions. Emit a log line per key event with source/destination/transform so regressions can be caught with log assertions, not manual testing.
- 2026-05-14 [#tmuxe2etests] #agent-doc-bug: Create end-to-end tmux input test harness that launches real tmux sessions (in CI-safe headless mode) and verifies Enter, arrow keys, and prompt submit through the actual supervisor→tmux→harness pipeline. Current SimWorld tests verify logic but not the tmux IPC surface.
- 2026-05-14 [#clearinterruptvim] #agent-doc-bug: Clear Session Context interrupt-clear UX can still land the operator in vim or a confusing terminal state after the explicit discard confirmation; tighten the interrupt-clear path so it exits/avoids editor prompts, waits for a real harness-ready prompt or clean closed state, and reports a precise recovery action instead of leaving the pane in an editor.
- 2026-05-14 [#opsreadinessrace] Tighten route readiness handling: avoid dispatch/clear attempts while the authoritative actor is `starting`; surface a single actionable wait/retry state until `prompt_ready=true`.

- 2026-05-21 [#jpktscope] [recommended] `agent-doc jobs create` derives the packet set from `agent-doc plan`'s `pending_mutations`, not from the full `repo_actions` text or `prompt_target` set. Observed 2026-05-21 dogfood: a session prompt `do [#x63e] [#v4v0] ...` produced exactly one packet (#x63e) because only x63e was a `pending_mutation` in the planning record. Result: queued items that aren't already backlog-tracked silently drop out of the job-packet workflow. Fix: have `jobs create` expand from the union of `pending_mutations` ∪ `prompt_target` ids referenced in `repo_actions`, or document the constraint that only `pending_mutations`-tracked ids are dispatchable.
- 2026-05-21 [#jpktwsmis] Job packet `write_scope` mis-attribution for cross-repo session documents. Observed 2026-05-21: dogfood against `session-share/tasks/root.md` produced a packet for `#x63e` (scope: `build-party/specs/convex-knowledge-layer.md`) with `write_scope: ["src/agent-doc/specs/", "src/agent-doc/src/", "src/agent-doc/tests/", "src/tsift/"]` — the agent-doc/tsift defaults rather than the build-party path the backlog item names. Worker honoring this scope would refuse to touch build-party. Fix: derive `write_scope` from the backlog item's file references (use path-extraction heuristic over the item text) instead of defaulting to the document's own repo.
- 2026-05-21 [#jobslock] `agent-doc jobs create` fails when `tsift graph-db status` returns the SQLite hot-journal/read-only error (recurrence of `#w25k`). Observed 2026-05-21 second-cycle `#dogfood-tsift` run: `agent-doc jobs create tasks/software/tsift.md --audit` exited with `plan has blocker(s); refusing to create jobs: tsift graph evidence failed closed: ... Error code 5: The database file is locked`. Fix on the agent-doc side: when `tsift graph-db` is locked, fall back to a soft warning + `manual_packet_only: true` flag in the planning record rather than refusing job creation entirely. tsift-side root-cause fix lives at `tasks/software/tsift.md` `#gdblock`.
- 2026-05-21 [#missedpb] Missed-patchback false-positive in finalize / write --commit cycle. REPRO: prior turn's finalize lands a commit but the response body (patch:exchange block) never materializes in the file. Subsequent agent-doc finalize reports 'rejecting late fallback patch: cycle <id> already committed for <file>' AND agent-doc write --commit reports 'staged snapshot already matches HEAD for <file> — closing cycle as already committed' + 'detected post-commit local drift', yet session-check correctly detects 'live exchange ends with unresolved prompt-only closeout tail and no assistant response'. Net: state machine thinks the cycle closed when the visible response was never inserted. WORKAROUND: direct Edit + git commit. EVIDENCE: tasks/root.md cycle on 2026-05-21 ~17:23 EDT, commit 521ab300 in session-share landed a finalize commit with NO response body in the file; required manual repair via commit 98ff9008 ('agent-doc(root): manual repair — inject missing response body'). FIX PLAN: (1) in write --stream, detect when IPC delivery completed but the patch:exchange materialization step was skipped (sidecar normalization fallback path) and surface a hard error instead of silently committing; (2) in commit/finalize, treat 'snapshot already matches HEAD' as success ONLY when the snapshot itself contains the expected response body — currently the check passes on equality without verifying the response is present; (3) when session-check INTERRUPTED is raised post-commit, automatically open a write --commit-repair cycle that re-delivers the response from the last captured cycle state instead of returning success. DO NOT IMPLEMENT THE FIX.

- 2026-05-25 [#adoc-compact-during-turn-response-loss] agent-doc-bug: when a JetBrains-initiated agent-doc compact exchange (or any other binary-owned commit on the same doc) lands between agent-doc preflight and agent-doc finalize within the same skill turn, finalize's late-fallback gate fires 'rejecting late fallback patch: cycle <ID> already committed' and the response heredoc is discarded. The pending mutations from the same finalize invocation do land in the working tree, but the response body never crosses the patch boundary. Recovery today requires bare git commit + agent-doc reset --from-current. Plan: tasks/agent-doc/plan-compact-during-turn-response-loss.md. Concrete repro: tasks/agent-doc/agent-doc-bugs2.md cycle for #jbccc2 at 2026-05-25 03:42-03:45 EDT — preflight captured cycle cycle-1779694939132 at 03:42:30, compact exchange ran at 03:42:55 creating archive ...074255.md + commit 14bc483d3, finalize at 03:43 hit the late-fallback gate. Likely fix: finalize compares its CLI --baseline-file against current snapshot/HEAD; on drift, start a fresh cycle instead of short-circuiting on already_committed (Option A) or compact writes a sidecar marker so the next finalize knows to start fresh (Option B). Related: #adoc-jb-cache-cancel-stuck-cycle, #adoc-prefix-strip-uncommitted, #jbccfix.

- 2026-05-26 [#ignorebypass] Teach the finalize/write/commit auto-sweep to honor `.gitignore`. Today it stages via `git hash-object` + `git update-index --add` (porcelain-bypassing plumbing), so any file the session document references gets committed regardless of ignore rules. Concrete repro: with `tasks/.gitignore` listing `scratch/`, an agent-doc session that references `tasks/scratch/<x>.md` commits that file silently on finalize. Fix surface: pre-stage check via `git check-ignore --quiet --no-index` (or libgit2 equivalent) per candidate path before `update-index`; skip ignored paths with a visible diagnostic (or behind an `--include-ignored` opt-in) so users don't have to rebase-force-push to undo. Bug class: similar to #snapabsorb in that plumbing-level staging skips the safety net users expect from porcelain.

- 2026-06-03 [#prompt-preempts-auto-queue] [recommended] Unanswered exchange prompts must run before active `agent:queue auto` continuation, even when the prompt was accidentally baselined into the `.agent-doc` snapshot. Live repro 2026-05-30 `src/boost-client/tasks/monsterrodholders.md`: `What are #next-steps to complete review items?` was visible after the exchange boundary and duplicated in `agent:queue auto`, but the snapshot already contained the exchange prompt, so the next diff only saw queue bookkeeping and the prompt did not run. Fix: add snapshot-independent unresolved exchange prompt detection, make preflight/direct-run prefer it over queue synthetic diffs, prevent route queue snapshot recovery from absorbing unanswered exchange prompts, and defer Codex/Claude queue continuation while such a prompt exists. Plan: tasks/agent-doc/plan-prompt-preempts-auto-queue.md.
- 2026-06-03 [#queue-user-edit-overwrite] [recommended] User-authored agent:queue edits can be silently overwritten/deleted by write/reset/commit queue convergence. Live repro 2026-05-30 boost-client monsterrodholders.md: user added do [#gscaccess] at queue head; agent-doc repeatedly removed/reverted it during closeout/snapshot repair. Fresh repro 2026-06-02: operator wrote into agent:queue while the previous cycle was still running, and the closeout lost those queue changes without a fail-closed diagnostic. Fix by tracking queue edit provenance/epochs, rechecking the live queue at final visible-write/consume boundaries, preserving explicit queue edits unless the current response consumes that exact head, and failing closed on stale IPC/snapshot queue rewrites. Plan: tasks/agent-doc/plan-user-queue-edit-preservation.md.

- 2026-06-03 [#7az7] #clear-auto-queue-stall #agent-doc-bug 2026-06-03: JB 'Clear Session Context' runs agent-doc session clear → /new (OpenCode) or /clear (Claude/Codex) resets the harness conversation. After the clear completes, idle-queue-watch/auto-trigger fires on the still-active agent:queue auto component (queue_active: true persists in the document), injecting agent-doc <FILE> into the cleared session that has no agent-doc context → stall. Root causes: (1) no post-clear auto-queue suppression — idle-queue-watch and auto-trigger have no cooldown mechanism after session clear; (2) prompt_requests_clear() (codex_hook.rs:819-821) only matches /clear, not /new, so the route-level fresh-restart path does not apply to OpenCode sessions; (3) session clear (session_actor_cmd.rs:472-544) does not pause or mark the auto-queue. Fix: add a durable clear-cooldown marker (.agent-doc/queue-continuations/<doc-hash>.clear-cooldown) written by session clear, checked by idle-queue-watch and auto-trigger, cleared on explicit Run Agent Doc invoke. Also fix prompt_requests_clear() to recognize /new. Plan: tasks/agent-doc/plan-post-clear-auto-queue-suppress.md
- 2026-06-03 [#codex-403-429-fallback] #agent-doc-bug 2026-06-03: agent-doc in a Codex session received 403 Forbidden on WebSocket handshake to `wss://chatgpt.com/backend-api/codex/responses` (cf-ray: a05c18067af0dce4-IAD), then HTTPS fallback hit 429 Too Many Requests (request id: 3e924ff6-fde4-4fb2-8b00-08dfdd74e1a3). Other concurrent Codex sessions (e.g. agent-doc-bugs2.md pane) continued working. Possible causes: per-session rate limit, Cloudflare edge block on the specific connection, or session-specific token/auth state. Agent-doc should detect this pattern (403 on WS → 429 on HTTPS fallback) and (a) surface a clear diagnostic distinguishing transport rejection from agent-doc routing failure, (b) optionally retry with backoff or suggest restarting the codex session, and (c) not treat the 429 as a permanent failure that exhausts the retry budget without operator visibility.
- 2026-06-03 [#codex-network-access-opencode-guard] #agent-doc-bug 2026-06-03: frontmatter field `codex_network_access: enabled` is Codex-specific but agent-doc does not guard it when the session runs under opencode. When `agent: opencode` (or no Codex agent), `codex_network_access` has no effect — opencode does not have an equivalent network sandbox flag. Agent-doc should either (a) warn/ignore `codex_network_access` when the agent is not codex, or (b) not emit it into the dispatched prompt/session config for opencode sessions, to avoid confusion and prevent operators from assuming network access is controlled when it is not.

- 2026-06-05 [#route-ready-wait-fast-fail] [recommended] Improve fail-open-after-wait worst case (follow-up to #jb-monsterrod-route-ready-wait-stall). The ready-wait already early-exits when the pane is dispatch-ready (logs: route_starting_actor_ready elapsed_ms~100), so the full timeout only bites a pane that NEVER becomes ready. Levers: (1) fail-fast on a PROVEN-DEAD/closed pane — detect dead PID / closed pane / no session-log heartbeat and reroute to a fresh pane immediately instead of waiting the full dispatch_only_starting_pane_ready_timeout (route.rs:2245); keep waiting only for a still-booting pane (overlaps #route-starting-timeout-poll-recovery, #snrun). (2) per-harness adaptive timeout — Codex cold-start (auth+boot) is slow, Claude fast; tune to observed p99 instead of one flat 60s (timeout is already binary-scoped via _for_binary). (3) warm-pane pool so routing rarely waits for cold start (bigger). (4) push readiness signal vs 250ms poll (minor). Highest value: (1)+(2).
- 2026-06-05 [#codex-mcp-vs-cli-reinvoke] [recommended] DECISION: fix the Codex recursive self-shell-out at the root. Cause: Codex has no in-process skill execution, so it drives agent-doc by running the CLI (agent-doc <FILE>); continuing a turn = re-running the CLI, and the Stop-hook auto-loop does so inside the owner pane → the new process re-enters the pane it runs in → recursion (recursive_direct_invocation_blocked guard catches it). Option A (MCP server): expose the hot-path workflow as MCP tools + a session-holding server so Codex drives agent-doc via tool calls against a persistent session, never spawning a nested CLI in the owner pane — removes the recursion structurally; bigger build. Option B (#codex-self-reinvoke-prevent): keep the CLI, make owner-pane re-invocation continue-in-place instead of nested-dispatch (the deadlock guard is the detect half; this adds the redirect half) — smaller, tactical, guards rather than removes the recursion. RECOMMEND: A (MCP) as the durable fix if Codex is first-class, with B as the interim so the deadlock stops biting now. Needs user direction on A vs B before implementation.
- 2026-06-05 [#codex-self-reinvoke-prevent] [recommended] prevent owner-pane re-invocation of `agent-doc <FILE>` (Codex). The recursive-direct-invocation guard (run.rs recursive_codex_direct_invocation_diagnostic) correctly refuses + abandons the empty cycle, but it fires because something still RE-RUNS the entrypoint inside the owning pane (Stop-hook auto-loop, JB Run, or operator retry). True prevention: those re-invocation sources should detect this-pane-already-owns-this-doc first and route to an in-pane continuation (answer-in-turn + finalize/write --commit) instead of shelling out agent-doc <FILE> again. Builds on existing recovery items #codex-owned-pane-prompt-miss (#49), #codex-owned-pane-auto-queue-stuck (#45), #codex-owned-pane-prompt-miss-followups (#44); plans tasks/agent-doc/plan-codex-owned-pane-prompt-miss.md + plan-recursive-direct-invocation-abandon.md.

Tail appends and non-head edits already do NOT stall. Refinement: instead of stripping auto + forcing queue_active:false on a head edit, adopt the edited head as the new prompt and keep the loop armed so user edits continue rather than stall. CAVEAT: must not grab a half-typed head — gate adopt-and-continue on a settled buffer (no live_prompt_drift_after_preflight) and keep the pause only while actively mid-edit. Requested 2026-06-05.

- 2026-06-09 [#queue-completed-items-escape-below-component] Completed/struck queue items can be written BELOW the `<!-- /agent:queue -->` closing marker, landing inside the adjacent HTML-comment "parking lot" instead of staying inside the `agent:queue` component (struck, until reaped) or being reaped into `agent:done`. Repro signature: after a closeout, the queue component renders empty while N `- ~~:pushpin: [...]~~` lines appear in the comment block beneath it (invisible in rendered markdown = orphaned residue). Observed 2026-06-08 as 12 struck lines in the UNCOMMITTED working tree only — committed HEAD was clean — so this is post-commit closeout drift, not the deterministic strike path (`preflight.rs:4660` `q.replace_content` writes inside the component). Most likely a CRDT/boundary merge displacing the queue's struck content past the component's closing boundary into the neighboring comment; same family as #ipc-crdt-response-drift / R1–R6. Fix: queue strike/reap must keep struck items within the queue component span and reaped items in `agent:done`; add a boundary-displacement guard (reject/repair queue content emitted after `<!-- /agent:queue -->`) + SimWorld coverage. Mechanism not yet pinned to an exact code path — root-cause before fixing.

- 2026-06-24 [#ocdrainstall] OpenCode agent-doc active-queue drain stalls after each committed closeout. SKILL says "OpenCode currently has no auto-loop" which agents read as "stop after each cycle," but it means "no AUTOMATED mechanism — the agent must self-drive the drain in-turn." Result: the agent stops and forces operator re-invocation per head; compounded by deferring drainable heads as "redundant" (#drain-no-defer violation). Claude Code uses /loop; Codex uses agent-doc hook codex-stop + .agent-doc/queue-continuations marker; OpenCode has neither. - 2026-07-18 [#sk47] Phase 1 of the watermark work (id=retentionversion), prerequisite for the per-peer ack watermark item directly below. Give `state_events` a first-class monotonic version column per document instead of the three ad-hoc encodings retention parses today: `id` (per-database AUTOINCREMENT, NOT peer-comparable), version smeared into `event_id` strings (`turn-intent-checkpoint:<doc>:<cycle>:561:<hash>` sequence, `document-authority-<doc>-1784424488871912-<src>` micros, `crdt-recovery:<doc>:17:<hash>` generation), and `payload_json` fields (`model_revision`, `intent_id`/`target_hash`). Land this ALONE and testable before the ack table — it is mechanical and it is what lets the six fact-specific retention rules collapse into one generic delete-below-watermark. Substrate note: `op_log` already has a `(document_path, lamport, actor)` clock, but live data is 1120 rows / lamport 1-323 / a SINGLE actor 'user', so it is a single-writer counter today, not a multi-actor clock — extend it with actor discipline rather than introducing a second ULID scheme, but do not assume it is ready as-is.

- 2026-07-19 [#fzmutloss] #agent-doc-bug A finalize CRDT-convergence timeout can land the response but SILENTLY DROP its backlog mutation. Operator-reported 2026-07-19 on brookebrodack-dev.md: finalize timed out at 60s with repeated compare_and_swap_raced, the binary resumed and committed the response, but the --done mutation was lost so the item stayed [ ] and queued despite the response having landed; repaired manually with agent-doc write --commit --done. Violates the response-closeout-is-atomic rule (response + queue-head consumption + backlog/done mutations + snapshot + commit succeed as one transaction or none). Retained-intent replay must carry the backlog/done mutation set with the response capture, not just the response body. Likely the same root cause as #patchretryidem.
- 2026-07-19 [#donestrikeextra] #agent-doc-bug A single --done struck ANOTHER open item's queue head. This cycle passed exactly one --done (fzmutloss) plus a --backlog-edit on patchretryidem, and the write logged: '[queue] marked 2 completed item(s) by done id: ["do [#fzmutloss]", "do [#patchretryidem]"]'. The #patchretryidem BACKLOG item correctly stayed [ ] open, but its queue head was struck, so an unfinished item silently left the drain - the operator only sees it if they diff the queue. Suspect the done-id queue matcher is keyed off the same-cycle mutation set (which --backlog-edit joins) rather than strictly the --done id set. Restored the head manually this cycle. Regression coverage must pin: --backlog-edit on id B must never strike B's queue head when only A is --done.
- 2026-07-19 [#ctrliotestflake] agent-doc-controller-io has 2 order-dependent/flaky tests, PRE-EXISTING (proven: 3 runs on a clean stashed tree gave 1,1,2 failures). project_controller::rpc::tests::connect_or_launch_adopts_controller_published_during_launch_lock_contention (rpc.rs:14009) and project_controller::tests::dispatch_clears_stale_supervisor_pause_when_named_pid_is_dead_after_reboot (project_controller.rs:3889). Failure count varies run to run, so they share mutable process-global state. Make them deterministic (SimWorld or per-test isolation) so make check has a clean signal.
- 2026-07-19 [#ensurereplicagen] #agent-doc-bug The missing-replica wedge SURVIVES a successful re-registration. With #ensurereregister live (binary nudges, plugin 0.2.279 re-establishes), idea.log shows the full happy path per nudge: transport.register ok=true, register elapsed ~170ms, 'atomically replaced cached forwarder for agent-doc-bugs2.md', open-document-attach elapsed ~290ms force_refresh=true. Controller logs the matching crdt_replica_register bootstrap_bytes=290970 generation_fenced=true superseded_generations_pruned=1. Yet the very next observation still reads editor_attached_model_missing, AND CrdtReplicaManager.hasOpenDocumentReplica(file) still reads false (proven: the guard keeps letting the nudge through). So a replica is registered and its forwarder cached, but neither the binary reader nor the plugin's own forwarders map sees it. Two suspects: (a) generation fencing - each register fences the prior generation, and the reader may be resolving against a superseded/fenced generation; (b) path keying - forwarders is keyed by the plugin's RESOLVED file.path while the guard/reader use the binary's canonical path string. Start by logging both keys and the resolved-vs-fenced generation at read time.
- 2026-07-19 [#ensurereplicagensup] #agent-doc-bug Same process-local hub_registry class as #ensurereplicagen, remaining callers. Proof: document_model_ensure_failed for equityfundingsource.md with source=controller_recycle_request:background, and crdt_current_text_unavailable reason=missing_replica from process_pid 2761972/458607/503509 — supervisor pids, NOT the controller (3811498). hub_registry() is only ever populated by the controller, so any supervisor/CLI caller of current_text_for_file* / ensure_document_model / apply_cpc_write_for_file can only miss, permanently. #ensurereplicagen fixed the preflight queue-maintenance caller (proven: queue CAS now succeeds); this covers the rest. Audit every direct caller outside the controller and route through current_text_via_controller_model_for_doc / apply_cpc_write_via_controller_model_for_doc behind the embedded_relay_is_available_for_file guard, then EXTEND the test_cli CPC-boundary forbidden-crate list to cover them (agent-doc-preflight-io was missing from it, which is how the original escaped).
- 2026-07-19 [#crdtcasraced] Optimize CRDT convergence on the finalize path and take pressure off finalize with streaming salient responses. Operator-reported: 60s convergence timeout with repeated compare_and_swap_raced. Two directions: (a) reduce the CAS retry churn itself, (b) let semantic response checkpoints (agent-doc response-checkpoint) carry more of the response before finalize so the final seal converges a smaller delta. Relates to #realtimesalient. Pairs with #patchretryidem and #fzmutloss - those are consequences of this timeout path.
- 2026-07-19 [#crdtcaschkpt] #crdtcasraced direction (b), split out after (a) landed in 8cbb8ea4a. Take pressure OFF the finalize convergence window by letting semantic response checkpoints carry the response incrementally: complete '### Re:' sections already cross agent-doc response-checkpoint, so by the time finalize runs it should have little or nothing left to converge. (a) reduced the cost of a contended window (~2400 -> ~245 merge+CAS attempts); (b) attacks the window's SIZE instead. Design questions to settle first: which checkpoints are safe to publish mid-turn without ever exposing an incomplete token prefix (the existing rule is complete sections with balanced fences/markers only), how finalize reconciles an already-checkpointed response so it is not re-applied, and whether the boundary marker moves per checkpoint or only at the seal. Measure before/after with the same stalled-window attempt budget the (a) tests pin.
- 2026-07-19 [#crdtcaschkptdrive] #crdtcasraced direction (b), remaining half. The checkpoint mechanism is now SAFE to use incrementally (duplication fixed + regression tests, this cycle), and finalize dedupes an already-checkpointed prefix, but nothing DRIVES checkpointing: agent_doc_write_runtime_io::checkpoint_response has exactly one caller, the CLI subcommand, so in practice finalize still converges the whole response every turn. Scope this as an instruction-surface change, NOT another mechanism change: have SKILL.md/runbooks/streaming-checkpoints.md direct agents to run 'agent-doc response-checkpoint' as each complete '### Re:' section is produced (balanced fences/markers only, never a partial token prefix). Then measure: compare finalize convergence elapsed_ms and merge+CAS attempt count on a large document with and without checkpointing, using the stalled-window attempt budget the #crdtcasraced (a) tests pin. Open design question that remains: whether the boundary marker moves per checkpoint or only at the final seal.
- 2026-07-19 [#ctrlrespawnenoent] #agent-doc-bug The project controller can die during 'make install' and then fail to respawn. Observed 2026-07-19 on /home/brian/work/btakita/agent-loop: every agent-doc command failed with pre_write_guards -> editor_attached_model_missing, caused by 'failed to launch project controller (binary=/home/brian/.cargo/bin/agent-doc, spawn_attempts=6): No such file or directory (os error 2)'. The binary existed immediately afterward — cargo install had been mid-replacement of that path, so all 6 spawn attempts hit the ENOENT window and gave up. Recovery required manually starting 'agent-doc controller serve --project-root <root> --launch-mode lazy'. Fix: make spawn retry tolerate a transient ENOENT on the binary path (it is being replaced, not missing) with a bounded backoff spanning a realistic install window, rather than exhausting 6 fast attempts; and surface a clearer operator message than editor_attached_model_missing, which points diagnosis at the editor when the real cause is a missing controller.
- 2026-07-19 [#jbedtledger] JB plugin: move Run Agent Doc work off the EDT. RunAgentDocAttemptLedger.writeStage (RunAgentDocAttemptLedger.kt:122-161) does mkdirs + full readLines + PluginManager lookup + full writeText per stage, and 5 stages run synchronously on the EDT (SubmitAction.kt:31,49,59; TerminalUtil.kt:367,444). Also fdm.saveDocument on the EDT (SubmitAction.kt:58), and resolveProject resolves the same file twice via FFI (SubmitAction.kt:29 and TerminalUtil.kt:362) with the first result discarded. Needs a plugin version bump + rebuild.
- 2026-07-19 [#jbsockdeadline] #agent-doc-bug CpcRouteClient socket read has no deadline and the RUN_AGENT_DOC registry slot is never released on hang. SocketChannel.open (CpcRouteClient.kt:440) sets no connect/read timeout and reader.readLine() (:447) blocks indefinitely; the 15s is only a server-side deadline hint in the payload. A wedged controller hangs the route thread forever while the editorCommandRegistry slot (TerminalUtil.kt:447,372) stays held, so every later click dedupes out with no user-visible outcome. Likely the real mechanism behind 'Run Agent Doc does nothing'. Fix: real read deadline plus a finally that releases the slot.

- 2026-07-19 [#lazilynottl] #lazilynottl Replace the process-local TTL cooldown gating replica re-registration with realtime-model state. SITE: `REPLICA_REREGISTER_COOLDOWN` + `replica_reregister_cooldown_elapsed` (agent-doc-crdt-relay-io/src/lib.rs:236-260), consumed at :987 when an ensure observes `EditorAttachedMissingReplica`. DEFECT: a `static Mutex<HashMap<doc_hash, Instant>>` is process-local, and agent-doc CLI invocations are short-lived processes — each preflight starts empty, so it collapses bursts only WITHIN one process and does nothing across them, which is where the storm comes from. Its own comment says preflights arrive ~1/sec during a drain; those are separate processes. Same class as the reverted #orphandrain in-memory backoff. WHERE THE FIX BELONGS (investigated): `signal_crdt_replica_event_counting` sends `DeliverCrdtRemote` DIRECTLY to the editor pid over IPC and never routes through the controller, so there is no controller coalescing point; `replica_identity_registry()` and `global_liveness_plane()` are process-local too. The only long-lived owner of the realtime model on this path is the EDITOR PLUGIN, which already knows whether its replica is registered and at what generation. So: drop the Rust TTL and let the plugin ignore a redundant force-refresh (Kotlin change + plugin version bump; needs an IDE restart to take effect, so final proof is operator-gated). DO NOT substitute a state.db coordination lease — still a TTL, misses the point.

- 2026-07-19 [#deliveryackcut] [focused-cycle] delivery_converged() requires every LIVE member to have ACKed, so a zombie replica (registered + reported live, but no longer ACKing) wedges every write until timeout. Exclude a non-ACKing member from the convergence cut the way a disconnected one already is (crdt_relay.rs delivery_converged/is_live). Needs a staleness signal — is_live is a reactive cell with no ACK clock — and a threshold conservative enough that a slow-but-live editor is never dropped, since dropping one clobbers operator edits. - 2026-07-19 [#soloackzombie] [focused-cycle] #agent-doc-bug Single-editor zombie replica still wedges writes until the operator runs 'agent-doc admin reload-lib'. #deliveryackcut (6a7dbd448) retires a non-ACKing live member ONLY while another live member remains, because retirement takes live_count to zero, which flips the document to detached/disk authority — and a disk write over an editor buffer holding unsaved operator edits is the clobber the path exists to prevent. Fixing the sole-editor case is a design question, not a threshold question: it needs a way to hand authority off from an unresponsive editor WITHOUT a direct session-document disk write. Candidate directions: (a) automate the reload-lib replica replacement in-process at the ACK deadline (replace the replica rather than dropping it, so live_count never hits zero); (b) a distinct 'live but unresponsive' authority state that blocks disk promotion while still excluding the member from the convergence cut. Do NOT resolve this by loosening retire_non_acking_live_members' keep-one invariant — that invariant is asserted by a_sole_zombie_is_never_retired.
- 2026-07-20 [#relaylockpoison] [focused-cycle] Finish the parking_lot migration. 49dde1624 converted the three locks with proven failure evidence (relay hub/identity registries, reliable-sync liveness plane, FFI replica registry); ~60 std::sync::Mutex sites remain, so the workspace currently runs BOTH disciplines and a reader cannot tell which applies to a given lock. WHY: the workspace deliberately keeps panic=unwind so the cdylib's catch_unwind guards survive a replica panic in the host IDE (crash 2026-07-09: agent_doc_replica_apply_local -> SIGABRT killed IntelliJ), but that unwind passes THROUGH any live guard, so a poisoning mutex turns a survivable panic into a permanent process-wide outage - exactly what the guard exists to prevent. The codebase had already split three ways on this for the same lock family: 21 sites map_err/propagate, 20 unwrap/expect (PANIC), and 9 (watch_authority, ipc-io, sync-io, resync, pty, shared_writer, prompt-io) already do unwrap_or_else(|p| p.into_inner()), which IS parking_lot semantics spelled by hand. HOW: change the type and let rustc enumerate the sites - it is exhaustive and scope-exact. Do NOT use regex/sed (in one session regex ate inner match arms, hit three out-of-scope files needing git checkout, and truncated a function body). tsift edit-intents is Markdown-only today and ast-grep/comby are not installed, so compiler-guided hand edits are the tool. CARVE-OUT: the 9 Condvar usages in agent-doc-controller-io/src/project_controller.rs and agent-doc-ipc-io/src/lib.rs need a real port, not a type swap - parking_lot::Condvar::wait_timeout takes &mut MutexGuard and returns WaitTimeoutResult. ALSO: the 10 agent-doc-ffi replica exports still document '-1 on poison' / 'null if not open / poisoned'; -1 is now unreachable there, so those contracts are false and should be corrected in the same pass. Do NOT run cargo fmt --all (it reformatted 66 unrelated files in the prior pass); format only the touched files.
- 2026-07-20 [#resumenocontinue] #agent-doc-bug DONE 2026-07-19. agent-doc must never emit a harness continue-latest flag (claude --continue): it resolves to whatever ran most recently in the directory, which need not be this document's conversation - operator-observed attaching to an unrelated session. Resume resolves ONLY this document's own recorded id; an unknown id starts fresh and reports why (ResumeDegrade::NoRecordedId / HarnessUnsupported). Referenced by code comments in agent-doc-harness and start-runtime-io.
- 2026-07-20 [#resumestale] #agent-doc-bug DONE 2026-07-20. agent-doc start only passed --resume when typed, so #resumecapture only paid off via restart-supervisor / JetBrains, not plain start (reported live). Fixed to default resume-if-recorded with --fresh to opt out; verified live that claude --resume <bad-id> hard-exits 1 rather than falling back, so added a pre-launch transcript-existence check that degrades a verified-missing id to fresh before ever launching the doomed child (unverified harness layouts never treated as evidence of absence). Also fixed #resumeclaim ordering: the claim check ran before frontmatter resolution and silently protected only explicit --resume <id>, never the common default case; now resolves frontmatter first, then claim-checks.
- 2026-07-20 [#restartselfdefer] #agent-doc-bug DONE 2026-07-20. #restartselfpane over-corrected: it refused EVERY restart requested from the target document's own pane, which regressed the healthy path. With a live supervisor a restart is a blue/green drain-and-supersede (drains to the turn boundary, then execve in place, preserving child and pane, no dropped turn), so a self-request is safe there - the requesting turn finishing IS the deferral. The guard now fires only when no supervisor exists to carry it, which is the case that genuinely cannot work. Also answers 'interrupt, restart, resume the turn' in the negative: draining beats interrupting wherever available, and --resume restores the CONVERSATION not an in-flight turn, so re-dispatching would replay partial side effects against an open cycle preflight guards.
- 2026-07-20 [#restartbusyquit] #agent-doc-bug DONE 2026-07-20. Controller quit keys could not free a mid-turn harness pane: C-d only quits an IDLE composer, so a running turn ignored it and the restart timed out after 10s with a generic live_harness_quit_timeout. Fixed: operator_quit_key_plan_for_state interrupts first when the pane is busy, busy is detected from the live capture (not assumed - interrupting an idle composer is not always harmless: Codex C-g opens the external editor), and the busy plan composes the shared operator_interrupt_key_plan rather than restating its keys so the Codex C-g rule cannot drift.
- 2026-07-20 [#restartselfpane] #agent-doc-bug DONE 2026-07-20. An agent running `session restart-supervisor` for its OWN document is mid-turn by construction, so the controller was told to quit the session making the request - it could only ever time out, and the Escape it sent likely dismissed that command's own permission prompt. Fixed: refuse up front by comparing $TMUX_PANE against the document's owner pane, before any keys are sent, with a message naming the pane and pointing at another pane or the editor's Restart Agent action.
- 2026-07-20 [#boundarystableid] #agent-doc-bug DONE 2026-07-20. ROOT CAUSE of the boundary drift under #boundaryaccum1, and a sharper finding than 'carry-forward never takes': element.rs append_with_boundary DELIBERATELY minted a fresh id on every append ('the boundary is consumed and re-inserted'), so 162 snapshots of one document held 162 distinct ids. Its comment claimed this matched apply_patches_with_overrides, but that path mints only when the boundary was LOST (its snowball guard) - the two were never actually matched, and they mint different FORMATS (element.rs 8-hex via new_boundary_id, template.rs a full Uuid::new_v4). Because live and committed minted independently, they differed by exactly the marker line and no later cycle could settle it. Fixed: append moves the existing id instead of re-minting. Remaining follow-up: unify the two id formats and fold compact-io's resolve_boundary_marker onto the same helper.
- 2026-07-20 [#writestdinhang] #agent-doc-bug agent-doc write --commit blocks FOREVER on fd 0 when stdin is an open socket that never closes (e.g. a tool/automation harness that does not redirect stdin). Diagnosed 2026-07-20 by /proc/PID/task/*/wchan showing unix_stream_read_generic on fd 0 -> the caller's shell, after two ~15min hangs that I initially and wrongly attributed to CRDT convergence. Workaround is < /dev/null. Fix: bound the stdin read, or detect a non-EOF-capable stdin and fail fast with guidance, so an automation caller gets an error instead of an indefinite hang.
- 2026-07-20 [#autoinstalldeferstale] #agent-doc-bug A committed crash fix in a sibling dependency can sit un-promoted indefinitely. Proven 2026-07-20: tmux-router c883a0a (skip unreadable registry rows instead of failing the whole load) plus its regression test were committed, but every live session kept crashing on the pre-fix code because supervisor auto-install logs `auto-install deferred: src/agent-doc has uncommitted changes; waiting for the source commit before promoting a binary` and defers on ANY uncommitted change in src/agent-doc — including work unrelated to the fix. The operator sees a generic `stale_install` preflight warning that says the install predates local source edits; it does NOT say a fix already landed is not the code running. Make the deferral legible: when auto-install defers, name the commits (agent-doc AND patched siblings) that the running binary is missing, and surface that in the preflight warning rather than only in supervisor-stderr.log.
- 2026-07-20 [#orphandrain] [recommended] Controller-side drain for documents with NO supervisor. Shipped then REVERTED same session (revert of 'feat(controller): drain go-mode queues that have no supervisor') after it caused a dispatch storm: 15 dispatches for one doc in ~60s despite a 90s backoff, ~31 contending controller processes. Two faults, both in the effects half: (1) run_editor_route called from INSIDE the controller re-enters the controller ('timed out after 5.0s waiting for project controller response') — Run Agent Doc calls it from OUTSIDE; (2) the backoff lived in an in-memory HashMap in the controller, so every failed dispatch caused a restart that wiped it, permitting immediate re-dispatch. Redesign: dispatch from OUTSIDE the controller event loop (detached child, not a self-call) and persist per-document backoff in state.db next to the drain lease. The pure policy module (agent-doc-controller/src/orphan_drain.rs, 9 tests, gates correct) was sound — recover it from git history rather than rewriting. Underlying gap remains OPEN: a doc whose owner pane is a live agent TUI can never get a supervisor (preserve_pane_blocked), so it has no idle-queue watch and its go-mode queue only advances when a human triggers Run Agent Doc.

I checked: all 12 response sections are present and no operator text was lost. Disk and editor authority are currently diverged; per the tool that's status-only and self-healing, so I have a poll running rather than forcing anything. The code is committed and pushed independently of that.`
- 2026-07-20 [#jbrouteasyncchannel] [focused-cycle] #agent-doc-bug Move editor_route onto the async accept-on-admission endpoint — BLOCKED on a completion channel, do NOT just add it to the whitelist. Tagged [focused-cycle] because it needs a clean design cycle, not an in-loop drain: it surfaced as the drain head 3x and was correctly declined each time. Split from #jbroutasync. The mechanical change is small: validate_async_editor_command_payload (rpc.rs:5465) whitelists only sync_tmux_layout/focus_document_pane, so adding an editor_route arm would route it through handle_editor_command_submit_async_rpc. DO NOT DO THAT ALONE — it silently destroys every Run Agent Doc failure notification. resolveCommandSubmitAcceptedData (CpcRouteClient.kt:431) returns CpcEditorRouteResult(0, output) — exit code 0 = SUCCESS — for status submitted/accepted/running/applied. The entire classification chain in TerminalUtil keys off a NON-ZERO exit: BUSY_RUNNING -> notifyRunAgentDocStillRunning, QUEUED_PENDING -> notifyRunAgentDocQueued, RETRYABLE_STARTING retries, DISPATCH_START_UNPROVEN, and the catch-all notifyPersistentRouteFailure (TerminalUtil.kt:490-566). Accept-on-admission returns 0 immediately so none can fire, and every click would report success. This is the exact feedback path verified working 2026-07-19 when a busy pane correctly refused to inject on brookebrodack-dev.md. PREREQUISITE: a completion channel so the plugin learns the real outcome after acceptance (poll the command projection by command_id — the accept response already carries a projection with per-command status/terminal fields — or a controller->plugin completion event), with the classification chain running against THAT rather than the submit response. Only then is the latency win safe.
- 2026-07-22 [#orphanrouteworker] Serialize orphan-drain routing and recover supervisor restart prompts without fleet-wide resync
- 2026-07-22 [#libgcpidrace] #agent-doc-bug lib_gc::tests::gc_removes_lib_with_dead_pid_lock (src/lib_gc.rs:302, 'assertion failed: !v1.exists()') fails intermittently under nextest parallelism and passes in isolation and on re-run. Observed 2026-07-19 during the #ay80ci cycle: one make check run failed it, the next full run was 7804/7804 green. Dead-pid lib GC is racing another concurrently-running test's pid/lib dir, so the 'is this pid dead' probe sees a pid that is alive/reused (or the lib dir is recreated between the probe and the assert). Will redden CI intermittently the same way #ay80ci did, so pin it deterministically rather than retrying: isolate the lib root per test (unique temp dir keyed by test name) and/or make the liveness probe take an explicit injected pid-alive predicate instead of consulting the real process table.
- 2026-07-22 [#hubregistryevict] [focused-cycle] #agent-doc-bug agent-doc-crdt-relay-io's hub_registry (static Mutex<HashMap<String, RelayHub>>) is never evicted in production code — only tests call .remove(). Every document the controller ever touches leaves one RelayHub (its own ThreadSafeContext, canonical replica, member mirrors, liveness cell family) alive for the process lifetime. Found while proving #lazilyscopeadopt: this is the ONLY real process-lifetime growth in the reactive layer, and it is a registry-eviction question, not a ctx.scope() one. Eviction is not free: with_hub recreates an EMPTY hub on next contact, so a wrong eviction is exactly the phantom missing_replica shape. Sequence AFTER #missingreplicarebuild since eviction increases traffic on that recovery path. A missing hub is already a first-class handled state (CurrentText::EditorAttachedMissingReplica + recover_missing_hub_from_durable_projection), so the safe shape is evict-only-when-no-members-and-committed, verified by a test that re-contact after eviction recovers the same canonical text.
- 2026-07-22 [#authoritydescentstd] [focused-cycle] #agent-doc-bug Standardize the authority rebuild/descent order into ONE shared pure decision fn. IMPLEMENTED TWICE, independently, under the SAME tags (#px82/#bn41): (1) agent-doc-document-realtime-io reobserve_missing_editor_replica_with_reregistration + resolve_editor_unavailable_disk_read_fallback; (2) agent-doc-preflight-io current_text_via_preflight_authority_retrying + observe_current_text_with_bounded_retry. PROVEN DRIFT: separate budgets/env overrides (AGENT_DOC_EDITOR_REPLICA_REOBSERVE_ATTEMPTS vs AGENT_DOC_QUEUE_AUTHORITY_ATTEMPTS, both default 3 but independently tunable), and the liveness-witness memo from adefcda0a exists ONLY in the realtime-io copy (grep in preflight-io = 0) — a policy fix silently applied to half the paths. DESIGN DECIDED 2026-07-19: home is agent-doc-turn, NOT a new crate (every agent-doc crate is already publish=false, so a crate buys a boundary not privacy) and NOT agent-doc-document-realtime (preflight-io does not depend on it and would take a new edge onto write_policy.rs 161KB + crdt_relay.rs 107KB; both consumers ALREADY depend on agent-doc-turn, which already hosts closeout_recovery/cycle_policy/wait_machine/repair). SHAPE: follow the closeout_recovery.rs precedent — 'File/effect adapters classify concrete diffs into this smaller vocabulary before asking the turn policy to pick the recovery state.' Pure observation vocabulary (Current/Detached/MissingReplica/SyncPending/Error) + decision enum (Retry/RebuildFromPlugin/DescendToDisk/FailClosed); each -io site maps its own CurrentText in. The adapter is load-bearing, not ceremony: CurrentText lives in agent-doc-crdt-relay-io (effectful), so a direct dependency would invert the layering and make agent-doc-turn depend on an -io crate. CONSTRAINT: the two sites are similar, NOT identical — realtime-io gates rebuild eligibility on sync_pending vs missing_replica, preflight-io retries both. Preserve that as a parameter; flattening it reintroduces the ~28s-per-read-site double rebuild the exclusion was added to fix. Prerequisite for #missingreplicarebuild.
- 2026-07-22 [#missingreplicarebuild] [focused-cycle] #agent-doc-bug Read authority descends to DISK while an editor is attached, on the missing_replica path. The operator's directive — realtime model, then rebuild from the plugin, then disk — is ALREADY the structure: resolve_editor_unavailable_disk_read_fallback has an explicit 'Tier 1 retry: rebuild the model from the live editor buffer' calling ensure_document_model_through_authority. But it is gated `rebuild_eligible = reason == "sync_pending" && observe_editor_open(file)`; missing_replica is EXCLUDED because reobserve_missing_editor_replica_with_reregistration self-heals it upstream (excluding it was a real fix: rebuilding twice measured ~28s per read site). The gap is what happens when that upstream self-heal EXHAUSTS — the descent then reaches disk with an editor still attached, which is the corruption vector (operator-observed: frontmatter closing fence gained a leading space, deleted queue heads resurrected). Fix direction: on missing_replica exhaustion, do NOT descend to disk while observe_editor_open(file) — request one plugin rebuild (the forceRefresh/re-register the runbook describes) and fail closed if it cannot answer, rather than adopting disk. Depends on #deliveryackcut: a live-but-non-ACKing replica is why the upstream heal exhausts in the first place.
- 2026-07-22 [#patchretryidem] [focused-cycle] #agent-doc-bug Post-CAS-race finalize retry splices the document (duplicate '> **Queue prompt:**' blockquote + bare ' -->' partial terminator, #boundarysplice). NARROWED 2026-07-19 (427a01174): patch APPLICATION is ruled out — applying the same exchange patch twice through apply_patches_pure yields no marker debris, a single component terminator, and no duplicated operator prompt (regression: reapplying_the_same_exchange_patch_does_not_splice_the_document). The CRDT re-merge loop is also idempotent by construction. So the splice originates where the RETRY CONSTRUCTS the content it applies — the content_ours / response-tail assembly above the apply layer, not the apply itself. Next probe: capture content_ours on the second attempt of a raced finalize and diff it against the first; the debris implies the tail is being concatenated onto an image that already contains it. Live evidence is in tasks/brookebrodack-dev.md (owned by pane %9 — do NOT edit from another session).

- 2026-07-23 [#boundaryaccum1] [focused-cycle] #agent-doc-bug Scattered agent:boundary markers accumulate in committed content (3 in this doc: d0dd7c4e, qactsync1, ftimm0001) — violates the exactly-one-boundary invariant. Root cause: reposition_boundary_in_snapshot (git.rs:1613) hands the collapse to the finalize/IPC path via the active-run guard AND delegates the working-tree rewrite to the IDE plugin via the IPC-listener guard; when finalize wedges on stale_install and the response lands via direct commit (and the stale plugin never applies the IPC reposition signal), nothing re-enforces the single-boundary invariant on the binary's own staged/committed blob, so one boundary accretes per wedged cycle. Fix: always collapse boundaries in the binary-owned snapshot/staged blob independent of the guards (git stages a snapshot, not the live editor buffer, so it never races user text) and scope the guards to the working-tree disk rewrite only; add a post-commit invariant check that reconciles or fails loudly if HEAD carries >1 boundary; promote the aspirational reposition_boundary_removes_stale/apply_patches_boundary_invariant tests to real regressions + SimWorld coverage for N wedged-finalize→direct-commit cycles. Distinct from #fullboundary (convergence gate reduces the wedge trigger; this makes the invariant self-healing regardless of write path). Plan: tasks/agent-doc/plan-boundary-accumulation-committed-blob.md

- 2026-07-24 [#ftimmediate] [focused-cycle] #agent-doc-feature Free-text prompts begin work immediately — ROOT GAP IDENTIFIED 2026-07-19. The run-after-clear route the item wants ALREADY EXISTS for id-backed items: DrainScope::Supervisor defers only [operator-verify], and ExecutionContext::loop_undrainable's own doc says a [focused-cycle] item is deferred by the in-session loop but DRAINED by the supervisor clear-and-continue path (#qfocsup). The gap is that partition_drainable_backlog_ids takes backlog IDS and ExecutionContext is a HashMap keyed by id, so a FREE-TEXT head has no carrier for the tag and can never be routed there — leaving the agent to park it in prose, which is the reported behavior. FIX: give free-text heads an execution context (inline marker parsed off the head text, or a synthesized id at queue-materialization time so every head is id-backed) so an agent judging 'this needs fresh context' can route it to the supervisor clear-and-continue drain instead of handing it back. Note #multilineheadbullet and #qconsumenostrike point the same way: free-text heads lacking a stable identity is the shared root.

- 2026-07-25 [#convergedderived] #agent-doc-bug DocumentWriteConverged is an EVENT SOMEONE MUST REMEMBER TO EMIT, and that is the defect. Live wedge 2026-07-25: cycle-1785009796180 committed successfully (disk == live == HEAD), but no DocumentWriteConverged fired, so the pending_write_journal entry survived and retained_captured_response_write() stayed Some — which made handle_state_event_append (rpc.rs:10214) reject EVERY subsequent cycle for the document. doctor, session-check and repair all reported clean; only the state actor blocked. Root cause is that the retention predicate (state-backbone lib.rs:1230) compares two STORED values — capture.response_body against pending.target_content — and never against CURRENT AUTHORITY, so it structurally cannot notice the write already landed. Fix: derive convergence instead of trusting an emission. Computed over (journal entry, current authority text) via response_materialized_in_content, so a missed emitter self-heals on the next read; then an Effect gated on that Computed does the durable journal prune (#lzdurablesink: persist the settled epoch from an Effect). Not an event.

- 2026-07-25 [#stategraphjoin] #agent-doc-bug Reactive graphs are lifetime-typed; disconnected islands are an anti-pattern. Audited 2026-07-25: 50 sites workspace-wide call *Context::new() directly, and agent-doc-state-backbone alone held NINE state machines each minting a private ThreadSafeContext in its constructor — nothing in one graph could derive from another, invalidation never crossed a boundary, and a Computed created in such a context is Computed in name only. A shared context is necessary but NOT sufficient: a bare &ThreadSafeContext lets a cell join a graph with the wrong LIFETIME, and neither direction is caught at runtime (document cell in a turn graph is torn down at closeout and silently stops updating; turn cell in a document graph leaks across turns). DONE so far: DocumentScope/TurnScope/ProcessScope types added, all nine machines gained new_in(&DocumentScope), rule encoded in AGENTS.md, regression asserts the join is real by reading state back through the scope's context. REMAINING: migrate the other ~40 call sites off bare *Context::new(), and add an architecture guard failing on a new bare context inside a long-lived owner.

- 2026-07-26 [#sccurrentpass-conditional] resolve_current_document_content runs 8x per session-check at ~491ms each (3929ms of a 4742ms run) — it should be a lazily Source/Computed, not the hand-rolled #sccurrentpass HashMap memo. The memo already exists; what defeats it is that run_with_options_inner calls invalidate_current_document_pass FIVE times unconditionally, after each self_heal that *can* mutate the document whether or not it did. Reactive shape: a Source holding the document version/content-hash, a Computed resolving over it, so a self-heal that changed nothing does not force a re-resolve. Requires the self_heal_* fns to report whether they actually mutated (they currently return Result<()> with no change signal) — that signal is the real fix, the Computed is how it is expressed.
- 2026-07-26 [#syncownerreactive] #agent-doc-bug Owner resolution re-observes per call instead of deriving. Measured 2026-07-25 in ops.log while the pane swap was broken: sync_latency tmux_router elapsed_ms=45063 (budget 1000), gap_window_to_ownership 25620, prune_stash_panes 8027 (budget 250), window_resolution 4708, controller_actor_lookup 4297. Every decision re-shells ps/tmux, which is why observation_cache exists at all: it memoizes raw argv output, which is a cache, not a derivation (its invalidation is 'recreate the context', because there are no edges to invalidate along). Make pane observations Sources in a scope (see stategraphjoin) and the pane->document owner map a Computed over them, so a pane change invalidates the owner map instead of every caller recomputing it. Classification stays a pure function in command_line.rs; it is the OBSERVATION that belongs in the graph.
- 2026-07-26 [#closeoutterminalreactive] #agent-doc-bug Replace coordination timeouts with a subscribed terminal condition. The closeout terminal state (cycle committed + write invariant held) is already derivable, but every waiter re-probes on a clock instead of subscribing: CLOSEOUT_OWNER_LEASE_SECS (a guess about liveness), the write-runtime renewal at LEASE/3, the settle wait, and the retry backoff. #closeoutwaitchurn's lease-scoping (f4e9677cc) shortened the guess from 300s to 20s for status-only probes but did not remove it. TARGET: a Computed terminal-condition cell in the controller's per-document graph that waiters block on, so 'the closeout finished' is an event rather than something a caller rediscovers by asking again. Distinguish COORDINATION timeouts (lease, settle wait, retry cadence — all replaceable by subscription, because the fact is derivable in-process) from TRANSPORT deadlines (a socket that may never answer — genuinely needs a bound, and should stay). Also make the lease self-releasing on owner exit rather than expiry-only: the 2026-07-26 instance was a live pid holding a lease it no longer needed. Success test: no operator-visible guidance anywhere says 'retry' a command whose only job is to observe a condition.

- 2026-07-26 [#adwritesourceenum] #agent-doc-refactor Type the write-intent discriminant. DocumentWriteIntentProjection.source is a String and behavior already keys off it by prefix and equality in three crates - document-realtime-io gates post-commit reposition on source == 'post_commit_reposition' and starts_with('serialized_atomic_write'), write-ipc-io routes transport on starts_with('force_disk') - with no single place enumerating the values. Constraint: source is serialized into state.db projections, so the enum needs an Unknown(String) variant that round-trips unrecognized values rather than failing deserialization on an older or newer peer - a hard error there would wedge exactly the cross-version case this is meant to make safe. Keep the delta proof regardless: it covers operator rebases that no stage ordering can express.
- 2026-07-26 [#adopseventenum] #agent-doc-refactor Type the ops.log event vocabulary. 812 distinct event names are emitted as free-text format! literals and 310 call sites match on log text with contains(...), so producer and consumer share no vocabulary and a renamed event silently stops matching - the failure is invisible because the matcher just returns false. This is a correctness surface, not just tidiness: autofix scans the last N ops.log lines to plan repairs, doctor and session-check classify recovery state from these strings, and the convergence-playback and digest paths parse them. Introduce a typed event enum (or at minimum shared const names) for the events that are PARSED rather than merely logged - start from the ~310 contains() sites and work backwards to their producers, since typing all 812 emitters at once is churn without payoff. Deliberately out of scope: events that are only ever read by humans.

Widening repair_committed_historical_snapshot_drift is REJECTED with reason: it rebases onto HEAD only when the visible document normalizes equal to HEAD, and once the live file carries content beyond HEAD, adopting HEAD would drop exactly the operator content the guard protects. The real defect (opaque refusal) is FIXED — commit-io now prints the verbatim recovery commands. What remains is a UX call for the operator, not a correctness fix: auto-healing a state whose purpose is to stop and be noticed. Decide, then implement or close.
- 2026-07-26 [#crdtwaitsubscribe] #agent-doc-feature Replace the CRDT convergence poll with a subscription to the convergence cell. MEASURED: the wait loop issues ~2 controller reads per iteration and ~18 per write (6,983 write-path controller_crdt_current_text reads across 390 acknowledged writes), so an 11s wait generates ~18 round trips against the very controller it is waiting on - structurally the same self-feeding loop as the idle-watch storm fixed in 511527103/f170067bb, and a plausible source of the controller_model_backpressure that is 10% of all waits. NOT justified by poll oversleep: CRDT_WRITE_BACKOFF_POLICY is 25ms initial / 250ms max, which is <=2% of an 11,000ms wait - do not sell this fix on latency-per-sleep, sell it on removing generated load. Doctrine already says so (#closeoutterminalreactive: 'every waiter re-probes on a clock instead of subscribing'), and ThreadSafeContext runs Computed/Effect bodies with NO state lock held (verified: recompute_slot_now clones the closure, runs compute(self) unlocked, and takes the write lock only to publish), so there is no throughput objection. CAUTION: this is the write path, the one place where being wrong loses operator text - keep the deadline/retained-intent fallback intact.

- 2026-07-26 [#crdtwaitsplit] #agent-doc-feature Separate controller backpressure from CRDT delivery convergence in the write wait. ControllerModelBackpressure and DeliveryAckPending are different conditions with different remedies (back off the controller vs wait on the editor) but currently share one loop, one backoff schedule and one 60s budget, so the wrong remedy is applied to whichever one is actually blocking. Partial coalescing already exists and must be preserved: #crdtcasraced added the `advanced` flag so a non-advancing apply does not reset the backoff floor - without it a contended write pinned at 25ms for the whole 60s budget (~2400 merge+CAS attempts, each worsening the contention it retried). Land after #crdtwaitsubscribe, whose per-condition subscription makes the split natural rather than two parallel poll loops.
- 2026-07-26 [#sessioncheckguardsweep] #agent-doc-bug session_check is the second-largest closeout phase - a flat 2.9-4.2s, observed once at 11,271ms - and it is a per-invocation ~30-guard sweep. Measured attribution from one run: inspect_with_warnings 2665ms (the nesting parent), guard_partial_staging_closeout 817ms, inspect_core 607ms, self_heal_response_replay_duplication 385ms, resolve_current_document_content 350ms/13x. The partial-staging guard is the concrete target: companion_findings iterates candidate_repos, which on this superproject enumerates every dirty submodule and then runs several git diffs per repo. candidate_repos legitimately needs --ignore-submodules=none (it is hunting dirty submodule repos, 341ms) and narrowing it with --untracked-files=no was measured at only 13ms and discarded, so the real question is a SEMANTICS decision: must a WARN-only narrow guard run on every closeout, or only when the cycle actually staged something beyond the session document? Related: #closeoutgitsubmodulescan (9e7ce2f79 fixed two 47x submodule scans in tracked_modified_paths that were NOT the dominant cost).
- 2026-07-26 [#crdtdeliverybatch] #agent-doc-feature [recommended] Opportunistic client-side batching of CRDT deliveries. GATED ON #ackeditorstamps - do not start this until the profile says it applies. Current evidence points the other way: the measured shape is ONE delivery waiting a long time for a single ACK (delivery_ack_pending is 457 of 522 waits), and batching improves throughput across many writes rather than the latency of one, so it would not shorten the wait we actually have. It becomes the right lever only if the per-state profile shows delivery_ack_pending accumulating as many short delivery cycles instead of one long stretch. Filed to keep the option recorded with its precondition, not as agreed work.
- 2026-07-26 [#closeoutgitsubmodulescan] #agent-doc-bug PARTIAL 9e7ce2f79 - closeout latency is dominated by git_commit (4.6-22.5s across eight measured cycles; everything except it and session-check is ~50ms). Fixed two whole-tree submodule scans in tracked_modified_paths: git status now passes --ignore-submodules=all (284ms -> 6ms on 69 submodules, and the outputs differ by exactly the gitlink lines #side-effect-exclude-submodules already discards), and submodule_paths reads .gitmodules via git config --get-regexp instead of running git submodule status (154ms -> ~0ms). REMAINING AND UNATTRIBUTED: those two save ~432ms per call and tracked_modified_paths has few callers, so they are NOT the bulk of a 5-22s git_commit. Next step is per-subprocess instrumentation inside the commit path (candidate: candidate_repos in partial_staging.rs, which legitimately passes --ignore-submodules=none and costs 341ms, and whether that guard needs to run on every closeout at all). Also unaddressed: session_check is a flat 2.9-4.2s, with inspect_with_warnings measured at 2665ms of it - a ~30-guard sweep run per invocation.
- 2026-07-26 [#replicareloadincremental] #agent-doc-bug CORRECTED 2026-07-26 - originally filed as THE cause of commit_authority latency; the data does not support that. Measured across 522 convergence waits: delivery_ack_pending 457 (88%), controller_model_backpressure 53 (10%), compare_and_swap_raced 12 (2%). commit_authority is 11-15s of a 15-17s closeout and it is the EDITOR confirming delivery. Correlation test: only 64/210 waits had a crdt_replica_register within 8s, so full-bootstrap-on-reload explains about 30% - real and worth fixing, but a MINORITY factor, not the cause. Ruled out by measurement: git (commit-io internal timer 9-19ms, plumbing 1-4ms each), the controller baseline round trip (record_committed_baseline 72-88ms), and poll oversleep (CRDT_WRITE_BACKOFF_POLICY is 25ms initial / 250ms max). REMAINING ~70% UNEXPLAINED. NEXT STEP IS INSTRUMENTATION, NOT A FOURTH HYPOTHESIS: decompose the ACK round trip with editor-side timestamps - plugin receives intent, applies to buffer, emits visible receipt, binary observes it. Two wrong guesses on this class today (supervisor status snapshot, controller watchdog tick) each cost a build+install+recycle and both collapsed immediately once a log line carried an attribution field. DO NOT background commit_authority to hide this: it is already the slow-path fallback, and making it the default turns 'captured but not committed' into the normal state - the gap every wedge repaired today lived in - and lets queue item N+1 start while N commits, which is the concurrent-cycle splice generator.
- 2026-07-26 [#adreplacementturndispatch] #agent-doc-bug Operator-reported 2026-07-26: interrupting a running Run Agent Doc turn, editing the prompt, then starting a new Run Agent Doc fails to converge, and realtime steering is rejected in the same window. Two exact errors: (1) 'failed to converge queued dispatch for tasks/professional/job-offers.md through editor IPC/disk: route_dispatch_queue: editor delivery ACK recovery did not settle within 8000ms and the exact canonical target lacks active retained-delivery proof; refusing closeout'; (2) 'Could not steer the active turn: authoritative supervisor rejected selected-text steering: turn steering requires an authoritative actor with an active busy turn'. OPERATOR'S STATED FIX: always send `agent-doc <file>` for BOTH starting a replacement turn in this scenario AND realtime steering (whether by text selection or diff), rather than a distinct dispatch path that can fail to converge. Note (2) is the same shape as #steergateidle, already in backlog: steering gated on an active busy turn and rejecting otherwise, where an idle pane should just dispatch. (1) is separate - a replacement turn after an interrupt has no retained-delivery proof for the edited prompt, so the 8s ACK recovery window expires and the dispatch is refused instead of falling back to the plain trigger.
- 2026-07-26 [#pauseddeletetombstone] #agent-doc-bug Paused-cycle reconciliation must preserve operator deletions of struck agent:queue items. If an agent is paused (for example because the model is at capacity), the operator deletes struck queue items, and the agent later continues, closeout reconciliation must happen entirely inside the agent-doc binary and must not re-add those deleted items from an older cycle snapshot. Add a regression reproducing the lazily.md convergence-race/no-op-closeout sequence and make live operator deletions authoritative.
- 2026-07-26 [#preflightreactive] #agent-doc-feature Decompose preflight into derived reads plus signal-gated effects instead of a fixed imperative step sequence. Today 'agent-doc preflight' runs step 0 layout check, step 1 repair, step 2 commit, step 3 claims, step 3c related docs, step 4 diff, then queue convergence and baseline checkpoint, in that order, and the ordering is correct only by convention — a step inserted in the wrong place breaks it silently, which is the same failure class as #preflightsettleparity and #closeoutwaitchurn. The steps split cleanly: (a) DERIVABLE READS — diff vs baseline, queue convergence view, effective/required/suggested tier, agent_model, claims, related docs, session accretion — are pure functions of (document, baseline, config) and belong in the controller's document scope as Computeds, continuously current, with the agent reading a projection; (b) ORDERED EFFECTS — repair, commit-of-the-prior-cycle, baseline checkpoint, cycle-open — stay effects but become gated on derived signals rather than positionally ordered, so ordering is a data dependency instead of a call order. Cycle-open specifically does NOT become a Computed: it is an exactly-once durable transition carrying an identity (cycle-<id>) that the interrupted-cycle guard depends on, so it stays a state-machine transition. Success test: no preflight step can be skipped by a caller that forgot it, and inserting a new step cannot silently break an existing one's ordering. DEFERRED 2026-07-26: selected as the queue head but not worked this cycle - a standing operator goal (agent-doc plugin performance in IntelliJ and tmux) took precedence and consumed the turn. Item is unchanged and still fully actionable; nothing was partially landed against it.
- 2026-07-27 [#compactcachedeletetombstone] #agent-doc-bug Compact Exchange on `tasks/software/lazily.md` hit a File Cache Conflict, compacted the exchange, and resurrected operator-deleted struck queue rows. Keep reconciliation entirely inside the agent-doc binary, make durable operator deletes authoritative through compaction/cache-conflict recovery, and add an end-to-end regression.
- 2026-07-27 [#closeoutwaitchurn] #agent-doc-bug A held foreground closeout tells the agent a fact, not what to do, so agents invent a poll loop. Observed 2026-07-26 on tasks/software/lazily.md: one write turn spent 8+ background commands on 'wait then read settle output', 're-run session-check after recycle', 'poll settle output again', plus 'admin recycle' and 'admin reload-lib' — all self-invented recovery. ROOT CAUSE (grounded): agent-doc-closeout-runtime-io/src/lib.rs:75 returns CloseoutOwnerClaimOutcome::HeldByOther as the bare string 'foreground closeout owner {id} pid={pid} role={role} remains active until {expires}' with NO next command, unlike every other fail-closed path (cf. agent-doc-turn/src/closeout_recovery.rs:391, which names the exact command). CycleSuperseded is the same shape. And 'agent-doc session-check' has no blocking wait — its only options are --codex-final-gate and --help — so 'wait for the owner' is not expressible and the agent must re-invoke, choosing its own interval. Each re-invocation pays the full ~30-guard sweep (measured 4.1s, inspect_with_warnings 2.8s of it) to answer one question: is the lease still held. FIX: (1) add 'session-check <FILE> --wait[=secs]' that blocks on the closeout-owner lease / terminal cycle phase, defaulting to CLOSEOUT_OWNER_LEASE_SECS, with a cheap owner-lease-only probe that skips the guard sweep while the owner is still held; (2) make HeldByOther and CycleSuperseded emit a directive — the one command to run, the known deadline from owner.expires_secs, and an explicit 'do NOT run admin recycle / admin reload-lib / write --commit: they perturb the capture you are waiting on'; (3) align SKILL.md and runbooks/commit.md so the shipped instruction says 'wait once with --wait' instead of 'retry session-check', which reads as 'poll'. Regression coverage: a held-lease session-check must return the directive text and must NOT be satisfiable by repeated bare invocations in the test harness.
- 2026-07-27 [#sessioncheckturnreactive] #agent-doc-bug Track the turn reactively and supersede outstanding checks instead of requiring a separate session-check command. session-check exists as a distrust check on the AGENT: per AGENTS.md it is the direct-exec post-write guard that fails closed on an open cycle, a prompt-only exchange tail with no assistant response, or a likely direct assistant patchback that bypassed the binary write path. That is a real invariant, but it is enforced by a command the agent must remember to run — and which takes a closeout lease, can wedge, and whose own refusal text says 'retry only session-check'. Two changes: (1) the invariant is a property of the turn, and the NEXT turn's preflight already resolves the document, so it can evaluate the same guards without a separate agent-invoked step; (2) an outstanding check for turn N is MOOT once turn N+1 opens — supersession must cancel it and release its lease rather than leaving a dead claim to expire on a clock (the 2026-07-26 wedge was exactly a stale claim from a superseded probe). Keep session-check as an explicit command for direct-exec harnesses and debugging; remove it from the hot path. Related #closeoutterminalreactive, #preflightinbinary.
- 2026-07-27 [#backlogqueuepopulation] Make backlog-to-queue population binary-owned: when backlog mutations add or ungate actionable items, reconcile the explicit Queue component in dependency order in the same closeout; do not leave only an answered triage prompt or require a manual repair cycle.
- 2026-07-27 [#steergateidle] #agent-doc-bug Selected-text turn steering is gated on an active turn and rejects otherwise ('requires the authoritative pane to have an active turn (actor_state=ready)'). Both halves are wrong per operator: (a) an active turn should not be required — an idle pane should dispatch the selection as a normal trigger, not error; (b) detection was incorrect, a turn WAS active while it reported ready. (b) is corroborated by repeated 'timed out after 5.0s waiting for project controller response' on admin inspect in the same window, so a failed actor-state read may be surfacing as authoritative 'ready'. Fix both: idle becomes a dispatch path, and a failed read fails closed or retries instead of reporting ready. Related #selectedturnsteering.

- 2026-07-28 [#0dsr] [focused-cycle] PRINCIPLE: Run Agent Doc must recover ANY incomplete operation, not just the one that failed. Tagged [focused-cycle] 2026-07-19 — this is WRITE-PATH work whose failure modes are destructive (replaying a stale intent clobbers operator text; broken idempotency double-archives a compact), so it needs fresh context, not a mid-drain slot. MAP (the expensive part, traced 2026-07-19): (1) durable journal = pending_write_journal, agent-doc-state-backbone/src/lib.rs:1433 — deduped by intent_id, entries cleared on DocumentWriteConverged; (2) accessor = pending_document_write_journal, agent-doc-document-realtime-io/src/lib.rs:2824; (3) EXISTING replay = deferred_document_write_reconnect_content, same file :3115 — but triggered only by editor RECONNECT (:1214) and closing cut (:3571), which is the actual gap: a retained intent waits for a reconnect that may never come; (4) settle entry points already exposed to preflight via its effects = settle_retained_committed_projection / settle_retained_captured_projection, agent-doc-closeout-runtime-io/src/lib.rs:170/226/289; (5) the repair step to hook = agent-doc-preflight-command-io/src/run.rs:296, which runs on every Run Agent Doc. IDEMPOTENCY NOTE: intent_id dedup gives you 'do not replay the SAME intent twice' for free, but it does NOT prove replaying a STALE intent over newer content is safe — that guard still has to be designed and is the real risk. Triggering report: compact on tasks/professional/equityfundingsource.md failed with 'CRDT convergence did not settle within 60000ms' and left work retained with no operator surface to resume it.

- 2026-08-02 [#compacttombgc] [recommended] Fenced Compact Exchange CRDT epoch — RE-SCOPED 2026-07-19 away from tombstone GC. The #bootstrapsize measurement (crdt_sync.rs) on a real 57KB document found 88,630 ops and ZERO tombstones, so TextCrdt tombstone GC would reclaim nothing on the observed shape; the payload is pre-compaction INSERT history. The valuable half is the epoch itself: rebootstrap attached replicas from the compacted snapshot and reject/component-rebase older-epoch deltas, which discards that insert history — the only mechanism that makes compaction actually shrink the register bootstrap. Keep tombstone GC as a trailing step for delete-heavy documents, not the headline. Still needs the all-replica stability frontier before any epoch fence is safe.
