- 2026-08-02 [#multilineheadbullet] [focused-cycle] Make multiline `---` queue prompts addressable — SHARPENED 2026-07-19. Damage is already PREVENTED (#qconsumenostrike fail-closed guard, 5b3761dfc); this is the remaining correctness half. Refined finding: the ENTRY model already handles multiline correctly — document_queue::parse surfaces the multiline head as a Prompt (preflight lists it), mark_first_n_prompts_completed targets it, and render emits Completed multiline as `~~~done` fences. The mismatch is that markdown_ast::item_nodes (used for IPC node ops and AST-keyed consume) enumerates only list items, so it returns the NEIGHBOUR for the same logical head — observed as node_ops=[queue:1:neighbour:0] while consumed_text was the multiline head. So the fix is to reconcile the two ENUMERATORS, not the entry model: either teach item_nodes the `---` block shape, or render multiline heads with a bullet + indented continuation that both round-trips through parse_spans and yields a node. Needs one clean reproduction to confirm which of the two paths actually mutated the document in the observed strikes.
- 2026-08-03 [#percellconverge] #percellconverge #agent-doc-feature Per-component convergence: a turn must require only the components it OWNS to be settled, so the queue drains while the operator is live-editing an unowned component and that edit is never overwritten. Plan: tasks/agent-doc/plan-per-component-convergence.md. Root cause shared by the 2026-08-03 family that 0.35.120/0.35.121/0.35.123 each patched at the edge: every convergence gate compares the WHOLE document (editor authority vs disk), which can never hold while a buffer is being typed into, so live editing starves the drain by construction. op_log recorded ZERO per-item ops for a sixteen-item operator queue paste, so reconciliation has only whole-document text and every guard above it inherits that granularity. INCLUDES THE WRITE-PATH HALF: crdt_relay_pending_refusal in agent-doc-git-io/src/live_buffer_guard.rs still tells every agent unconditionally that this is a deferral not a lost response and to NOT re-send, force disk, admin recycle, or reload-lib. Observed three times on 2026-08-03 on this document: write --commit printed that refusal while session-check, carrying the 0.35.123 fix, contradicted it with NO cycle open and NO capture retained, STRANDED not deferred. session-check was right every time and each was recovered with agent-doc commit, so the two paths disagree and whichever an agent hits FIRST is the wrong one. Apply the same ownership test to the write path, keeping the do-NOT-recycle guidance intact for the genuinely-owned case. Four shippable phases: (1) record per-component ops, asserting the sixteen-line paste yields sixteen queue-item inserts in op_log where today it yields zero; (2) per-component authority hashes with no behavior change; (3) scope the convergence gate to owned components only, the phase that makes the drain run during live editing and the phase that can destroy an operator buffer if the ownership set is too broad; (4) per-component commit that rebases onto concurrent operator edits. Ship phase 3 behind a default-off flag. Required before phase 3: a SimWorld test where the agent writes exchange while a simulated operator edits queue every tick asserting every keystroke SURVIVES, an over-claim test proving that survival test can go red, and a replay of the three real 2026-08-03 documents at their state_events hashes asserting none reaches a permanent INTERRUPT

- 2026-08-03 [#qfoldedhead] #agent-doc-bug An editor paste can fold N `do [#id]` queue lines into ONE list item, with the bullet of every line but the first replaced by continuation indent. Those lines parse as inert Freeform residue: never drainable, unreachable by queue consume/strike, and counted as already-present by the multi-id-aware backlog mirror, so the fold is self-perpetuating and silently swallows every id after the first. FIXED in 0.35.120: heal_folded_id_per_line_prompts re-segments the block in preflight queue maintenance, verbatim so an operator do prefix survives, reported as a queue_folded_head_recovered warning. - 2026-08-03 [#pcc3] #agent-doc-feature Per-component convergence phase 3: scope the convergence gate to owned components. This is the phase that makes the queue drain during live editing AND the phase that can destroy an operator buffer if the ownership set is computed too broadly, so it ships behind a default-off config flag. REQUIRED BEFORE IT SHIPS: a SimWorld test where the agent writes exchange while a simulated operator edits queue every tick, asserting every keystroke SURVIVES; an over-claim test forcing queue into the owned set and proving that survival test can go RED; and a replay of the three real 2026-08-03 documents (lazily.md, haiven-dev.md, agent-doc-bugs2.md) at their state_events hashes asserting none reaches a permanent INTERRUPT. Depends on #pcc2. Plan: tasks/agent-doc/plan-per-component-convergence.md
- 2026-08-03 [#pcc4] #agent-doc-feature Per-component convergence phase 4: per-component commit. Commit only the owned components, rebasing onto whatever the operator has since written elsewhere in the document, instead of writing the whole file. Non-goal to preserve: the agent still never component-merges a disk candidate under a live editor - per-component scope narrows WHAT must converge, it does not grant new authority to overwrite. TWO defects, do not conflate: (A) pane_count_mismatch retries forever because it will not close a pane hosting an active agent and the column set has no slot for it - it needs a terminal named outcome; (B) convergence compares expected/actual as ordered DOCUMENT lists and never checks that the hosting panes are in corresponding left-to-right order, so at 06:31:30Z it reported converged/synced with panes=[%77,%95] while %77 was at x=181 and %95 at x=121 (physically reversed) and %72 at x=0 was unmodelled - focus then picks column 0 faithfully and lands on the rightmost pane, which is the wrong-pane-focus symptom. DO NOT fix B by indexing focus into the physical pane list: in this state that selects %72, injecting into a different document's agent, which is worse. Both recorded projections can be replayed as fixtures without a live IDE

- 2026-08-04 [#editormodelmissing] [operator-verify] #agent-doc-bug editor_attached_model_missing permanently wedges every write needing editor authority (agent-doc-bugs2 gen=1518, equityfundingsource gen=1452; healthy docs are gen 14-82). NARROWED 2026-07-18: survives admin recycle x2, admin reload-lib, AND a full IntelliJ restart, so it is NOT a plugin model-rebuild problem as first filed. Root cause is a one-way latch in editor-attachment state: TransportProjection.editor_generation (agent-doc-state-backbone/src/lib.rs:3394) is SET on StateFact::OwnerGenerationChanged (line ~1985) and never cleared — there is no editor-detach/close StateFact in the enum at all. document_has_live_editor_transport (agent-doc-document-realtime/src/crdt_authority.rs:165) therefore returns MultiReplica forever once an editor has ever attached, with NO liveness proof, even though the sibling authority_from_liveness (line 122) exists precisely to demote a stale listener via pid liveness. The ONLY predicate that flips it false is TransportPatchPhase::ForceDiskFallback — which is exactly why --force-disk is the sole escape the operator ever found. Second candidate latch, same shape: observe_editor_open's durable reliable-sync receiver journal may replay a stale editor-open assertion across an IDE restart (resolve_closed_editor_disk_fallback_current_doc, agent-doc-document-realtime-io/src/lib.rs:3774 bails only when it reports open). DECISIVE EXPERIMENT before coding: instrument which of the two reports attached — log editor_generation + observe_editor_open together at the EditorAttachedMissingReplica branch (agent-doc-crdt-relay-io/src/lib.rs:696). Both present identically, and the fix differs by which. DO NOT ship a speculative authority-demotion change: wrongly demoting a live editor lets the binary overwrite an operator buffer, which is the exact failure every guard on this path exists to prevent. Also worth shipping regardless: admin detect reported 'No fleet anomalies detected' while two documents were hard-wedged — add an attached-but-no-replica detector so this is visible instead of silent.

- 2026-08-08 [#stalewatchpid] #agent-doc-bug Watch pid helpers used the raw process working directory, so `watch --stop`/`--status` from a subdirectory read and removed a different path than the daemon wrote — and the removal discarded its result, so the real pid file survived as an immortal stale record (observed haiven-dev file named a pid dead for three days). Pid ops now resolve the project root, remove_pid[_in] are fallible and report, and clear_stale_pid[_in] prunes an identity-verified dead record from the read side. - 2026-08-08 [#zombiechild] #agent-doc-bug SupervisorShared::child_alive() read /proc/<pid> existence, which is true for an unreaped zombie, so a dead harness child pinned supervisor_restart_admission and every stale recycle execve'd with preserve-child over a corpse (68 recycles in 27 min on haiven-dev.md; operator typing swallowed). Liveness now reads /proc/<pid>/stat state.
- 2026-08-08 [#queuemirrororder] agent:queue mirror ignores anchored backlog placement, and cannot be reordered afterwards. (1) --backlog-add-after X "[#y] ..." places #y after #X in agent:backlog but PREPENDS it to agent:queue, inverting the drain order relative to the backlog. SKILL.md states the mirror matches backlog order; that holds for --backlog-add (both prepend) but not for --backlog-add-after/--backlog-add-before, where backlog honours the anchor and the queue applies --backlog-queue-placement prepend independently. Reproduced in tasks/haiven-dev.md: backlog = [#reasoncoderegistry, #reasoncodeenum] (correct dependency order), queue = [#reasoncodeenum, #reasoncoderegistry]. The failure lands exactly when an anchor was chosen BECAUSE order matters. (2) There is no recovery: 'agent-doc queue sync' skips ids already present (reason: already_in_queue) so it is append-only, and --backlog-reorder does not cascade to the mirror. The only ways to move a queued id are --done / --backlog-gate, both of which assert something false about its state. Suggested fix: honour the anchor when projecting into agent:queue, and add a queue reorder (or make --backlog-reorder cascade). (3) Minor, same session: a metadata-only write (reorder, no code change) was rejected by the imperative-directive guard — 'requires concrete execution evidence or a concrete blocker; rejected status-only/meta response for do [#typeroutersponses]' — and left the cycle at response_captured. A pure reorder has no execution evidence to offer.
